Result: Outperforms open-source baselines in relative RPC refinement with lower geometric consistency errors and substantially reduced matching time on multi-date collections; demonstrates robustness to diachronic appearance variation and improved efficiency.

Conclusion: Appearance-aware integration of learned matching with global similarity-based pair selection improves GCP-free RPC refinement in multi-date satellite imagery, enabling more reliable and scalable geolocation across seasonal changes.

Abstract: Accurate refinement of Rational Polynomial Camera (RPC) models is essential for high-quality satellite image geolocation. In ground control point (GCP)-free multi-view pipelines, this refinement is commonly performed through bundle adjustment from automatically extracted image correspondences. However, conventional RPC bundle adjustment pipelines rely on handcrafted feature matching, which becomes unreliable in multi-date collections affected by seasonal, illumination, and land-cover changes. We propose an appearance-aware RPC refinement pipeline that combines learned local feature matching for season-invariant correspondences with global image descriptors for selecting visually compatible image pairs. This reduces redundant and error-prone matching while preserving the connectivity of the matching graph. Experiments on seasonally diverse WorldView-3 images show that our pipeline improves GCP-free relative RPC refinement over open-source baselines, achieving lower geometric consistency errors while substantially reducing matching time on collections with 39-42 views. By making RPC refinement more robust to diachronic appearance variation, our approach enables more effective use of multi-date satellite imagery.

</details>


### [74] [Mitigating Compounding Error via Video Representation Regularization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27036)
*Taiye Chen,Qi Zhang,Yisen Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Investigates long-horizon video generation drift in autoregressive diffusion-based world models; links drift to collapse of latent representations (erank); shows data scaling alone is insufficient; introduces video representation regularization (VRR) to stabilize representations and reduce error accumulation; achieves notable gains over Diffusion Forcing on VBench metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To understand why sliding-window autoregressive video generation drifts and why existing scaling fails; to identify a mechanism and develop a training method to improve long-term stability for video world models.

Method: Analyze internal representations during generation; quantify effective rank (erank) of latent features; correlate rank collapse with generation drift; compare training strategies; propose and evaluate video representation regularization (VRR) against Diffusion Forcing; assess on VBench metrics.

Result: Found sharp decrease in effective rank at onset of drift, linking representational degradation to long-term instability. Pure data scaling does not mitigate drift. VRR stabilizes latent representations and reduces error accumulation, achieving substantial improvements on Aesthetic Quality (AQ) and Imaging Quality (IQ) metrics in VBench (e.g., AQ: 38.65→55.56; IQ: 44.37→72.08).

Conclusion: Establishes a causal connection between autoregressive drift and internal representations, highlights scaling limitations for video world models, and offers a simple, effective regularization approach to enhance long-horizon video generation robustness.

Abstract: Video diffusion-based world models enable long autoregressive video generation for robotics, autonomous driving and simulation tasks, yet sliding-window autoregressive inference suffers from severe error accumulation that degrades frame quality over time. Although this phenomenon has been widely observed, the underlying mechanism of compounding error and how to achieve stable long-horizon generation remain largely unresolved. In this paper, we investigate the internal representation dynamics of video world models and discover that compounding error is tightly coupled with dimensional collapse of hidden representations. Specifically, the effective rank of model representations sharply decreases at the onset of generation drift, revealing a strong connection between representational degradation and long-term rollout instability. Furthermore, we find that pure training data scaling fails to boost model resistance to error drift, contradicting mainstream scaling paradigms. To address this problem, we propose video representation regularization, a lightweight training constraint that stabilizes latent representations and suppresses iterative error accumulation. Compared with Diffusion Forcing, our method achieves improvements from 38.65 to 55.56 and from 44.37 to 72.08 on the Aesthetic Quality and Imaging Quality metrics of VBench. Our work establishes the first connection between autoregressive video drifting and model internal representations, adopts erank as a quantitative metric for error accumulation, reveals counterintuitive scaling limitations for video world models, and presents a simple yet effective regularization strategy to improve long video generation robustness.

</details>


### [75] [Object Detection for Autonomous Driving in Chinese Rural Scenes: An Experimental Study on Real-Synthetic Data Mixing and Model Evaluation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27058)
*Danning Zhu,Ziyan Lin,Jing Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A real-synthetic mixed dataset for Chinese rural roads with 14-category objects; evaluates 13 detectors across real:synthetic data ratios; moderate synthetic (1:0.5) boosts performance (YOLO11m mAP@0.5 = 0.758); excessive synthetic (1:1) causes domain shift; long-tail objects like stalls remain challenging; provides guidance on model choice and data strategy for rural autonomous driving.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address data scarcity and generalization gaps in rural Chinese driving scenarios by creating a real-synthetic dataset focusing on region-specific objects and scenes; systematically study how different real-to-synthetic data ratios affect detector performance to inform model selection and data strategies.

Method: Create a mixed dataset by collecting real-world images from Weishi County, Henan, and generating parameterized virtual scenes in Unreal Engine. Define a 14-category taxonomic system including region-specific items (electric tricycles, LSVs, roadside stalls). Train under a unified protocol and evaluate 13 detectors (YOLOv5/8/11/26 series, RT-DETR-L) across three configurations: all-real, 1:0.5 real:virtual, and 1:1 real:virtual.

Result: Moderate synthetic data (1:0.5) enhances detection performance; YOLO11m achieves the highest mAP@0.5 of 0.758. A higher synthetic proportion (1:1) induces domain shifts that offset scaling benefits. Most models detect common local vehicles well, but long-tail/non-standard objects (stalls, rails) show perceptual bottlenecks.

Conclusion: Provides empirical evidence for selecting models and designing synthetic data strategies for rural autonomous driving perception. Demonstrates that a controlled amount of synthetic data can improve performance, while excessive synthetic data may harm generalization due to domain shift; highlights remaining challenges with rare objects and suggests directions for future improvement.

Abstract: Currently, autonomous driving object detection models face significant data scarcity and generalization challenges when navigating complex Chinese rural traffic scenarios. To address these limitations, we propose a novel real-synthetic mixed object detection dataset tailored specifically for Chinese rural roads and systematically evaluate the performance of 13 mainstream detectors under different real-to-synthetic data ratios, thereby providing empirical evidence for model selection and data strategy design in rural autonomous driving scenarios. Our dataset combines real-world images captured in Weishi County, Henan Province, with parameterized virtual scenes generated via Unreal Engine. To accurately reflect the unique realities of rural traffic, we define a comprehensive 14-category object system encompassing region-specific elements such as electric tricycles, low-speed vehicles (LSVs), and roadside stalls. Under a unified training protocol, we systematically evaluate 13 mainstream detectors -- spanning the YOLOv5, YOLOv8, YOLO11, and YOLO26 series, as well as RT-DETR-L -- across three data configurations: an all-real baseline, a 1:0.5 real-to-virtual mix, and a 1:1 mix. Experimental results demonstrate that a moderate injection of synthetic data (1:0.5 ratio) effectively enhances detection performance, with YOLO11m achieving the highest mAP@0.5 of 0.758. However, a higher proportion of synthetic data (1:1) introduces domain shifts that offset the benefits of data scaling. While most models reliably identify distinct local vehicles, significant perceptual bottlenecks remain for long-tail, non-standard objects like stalls and railings. This research provides crucial empirical evidence and novel insights for model selection and synthetic data strategies, facilitating the practical deployment of autonomous driving perception systems in rural areas.

</details>


### [76] [ScratchSim: A Procedural Synthetic Data Pipeline for Surface Scratch Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27065)
*Paul Julius Kühn,Saptarshi Neil Sinha,Tiago Kleist,Richard Hoffmann,Arjan kuijper,Michael Weinmann*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Synthetic data via BlenderProc with domain randomization enables scalable defect detection; fine-tuning on synthetic weights outperforms real-data training; mixed training helps under limited real data; applicable to edge devices; datasets/pipeline to be released after acceptance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Overcome scarcity of annotated defect data for industrial surface defect detection and enable scalable, on-device inspection.

Method: Procedural rendering pipeline using BlenderProc to generate large-scale, COCO-formatted synthetic annotations with configurable material appearance, camera modes, and domain randomization; evaluate four training strategies (synthetic-only, real-only, mixed, fine-tuning from synthetic) on two objects with different materials using three lightweight detectors (YOLOX, YOLO26, LW-DETR).

Result: Fine-tuning from synthetic weights consistently surpasses real-only training; mixed training mitigates performance loss under scarce real data; findings hold for both convolutional (CNN-based) and transformer-based architectures.

Conclusion: The approach enables scalable, annotation-efficient defect detection suitable for on-device industrial inspection; project will release code, 3D models, and annotated datasets upon acceptance.

Abstract: While automated defect detection such as the detection of surface scratched is an important aspect in industrial quality control, the scarcity of annotated defect data make this task challenging. This paper presents a procedural rendering pipeline that generates large-scale annotated synthetic training data using BlenderProc, with configurable material appearance, camera modes, and domain randomization, producing automatic COCO-format annotations. To show the potential of our approach, we evaluate four training strategies, namely synthetic-only, real-only, mixed, and fine-tuning from synthetic weights, across two objects with different material properties and three lightweight edge-deployable detectors, YOLOX, YOLO26, and LW-DETR. Our evaluation show that fine-tuning from synthetic weights consistently outperforms real-only training, and that mixed training effectively recovers performance under scarce real-data conditions, with findings validated across both convolutional and transformer-based architectures. The proposed approach enables scalable defect detection without the burden of large real annotated datasets, making it practical for on-device industrial inspection. The pipeline scripts, 3D model, and both synthetic and real annotated scratch datasets for a glossy toy Ferrari car will be made available through the project website upon acceptance.

</details>


### [77] [SciFigAlign: Scoring Scientific Figures by Fine-tuned Alignment of Visuals with Manuscript Evidence](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27066)
*Chuanzhi Xu,Zihan Deng,Huiqi Liang,Chengkun Yue,Zhanlin Cui,Pengfei Ye,Weidong Cai*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Introduces SciFigAlign, a fine-tuned multimodal scorer trained on 3,857 annotated scientific figures to assess Clarity, Relevance, Informativeness, and Structure; achieves strong evaluation metrics and outperforms LLM-based baselines about figure quality in manuscript context.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Scientific figure quality evaluation requires aligning visual content with the manuscript's argument; traditional IQA captures only perceptual quality, and generic VLMs or zero-shot LLM judges fail to integrate figure, caption, and surrounding manuscript evidence.

Method: Annotated dataset of 3,857 figures with four dimensions. SciFigAlign fine-tunes CLIP and SciBERT with per-modality cross-attention and CubeMLP fusion, using figure crop, caption, citing paragraphs, and light paper context. Optimizes SmoothL1 regression jointly with a within-paper ranking hinge loss.

Result: Macro MAE of 0.3524 and within-paper pairwise accuracy of 81.64% on the test set (n=396) under paper-level splits; 59% relative error reduction over the best LLM-as-judge baseline with MAE 0.864. Ablations show manuscript-grounded inputs, citing-context denoising, and ranking supervision are crucial.

Conclusion: Accurate scientific-figure assessment requires learned alignment between visual content and manuscript evidence rather than prompting alone; SciFigAlign demonstrates the benefits of manuscript-grounded multimodal grounding for figure quality evaluation.

Abstract: Scientific figure assessment in peer review differs fundamentally from general image quality evaluation: a figure must be visually legible, faithfully support the manuscript's claims, and communicate evidence with a clear visual hierarchy. However, if we apply traditional image assessment methods to scientific figure quality assessment, limitations emerge: classic IQA models capture perceptual quality or aesthetics but cannot judge whether a figure serves the paper's scientific argument; CLIP-based methods assess generic image-text correspondence, yet lack understanding of manuscript context; and zero-shot LLM/VLM judges, when repurposed for figure scoring, often yield overly concentrated scores with limited fusion of visual and textual evidence. We introduce an annotated dataset of 3,857 scientific figures from peer-reviewed conference papers, each rated along four peer-review-oriented dimensions: Clarity, Relevance, Informativeness, and Structure. We propose SciFigAlign, a fine-tuned multimodal scorer that grounds figure quality assessment in manuscript evidence. Given a figure crop, caption, citing paragraphs, and light paper context, SciFigAlign fine-tunes CLIP and SciBERT end-to-end with per-modality cross-attention and CubeMLP fusion, jointly optimizing SmoothL1 regression with a within-paper ranking hinge loss. Under paper-level splits, SciFigAlign achieves a macro MAE of 0.3524 and a within-paper pairwise accuracy of 81.64% on the test set with n = 396, a 59% relative error reduction over the best LLM-as-judge baseline with MAE 0.864. Ablations confirm that manuscript-grounded inputs, citing-context denoising, and ranking supervision are all critical, showing that scientific figure assessment requires learned alignment between visual content and manuscript evidence rather than prompting alone, even with state-of-the-art VLMs.

</details>


### [78] [Visual Credit Audit for Multimodal Spatial Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27069)
*Feixiang Liu,Qiang Qiu,Lanbo Sun,Nan Wei,Huawei Shen,Xueqi Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: VCA (Visual Credit Audit) decomposes closed spatial benchmarks into three components: correctness, image-driven support, and relation-consistent response. The study finds a sizable fraction of correct decisions that are uncredited, demonstrates the influence of relation-specific visual evidence, and uses permutation/null-controls to isolate this effect.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To determine whether image context or relation-specific visual evidence actually drives model decisions in spatial benchmarks, separating true model understanding from exploitation of cues or implicit baselines in text-only or blank controls.

Method: Use fixed forced-choice spatial benchmarks with yes/no answers, introduce Visual Credit Audit (VCA) to separate two estimands: training/label-free image support versus text-only/blank controls, and relation-specific evidence responsiveness. Apply labels to derive dependency-credited correctness (D-CC). Use matched same-split image permutation, fixed-pixel relation contrasts, and a 3x3 evidence-source factorial. Assess on four open multi-language LLMs (MLLMs) and two spatial benchmarks; include controlled correct-but-uncredited agreements and a geometry-compatible edit audit (108 cases).

Result: 12.73–26.25% of decisions are correct but uncredited. Matched same-split image permutation reduces D-CC by 21.25–47.80 points (all 95% intervals > 0). Among controlled correct-but-uncredited agreements, relation reversal responses span 81.57%–100% and 32.11% show pooled change in answer. Independent geometry-compatible edits (108 cases) bound a natural-image correspondence check. Overall, VCA decomposes benchmark success into correctness, image support, and relation-consistent response.

Conclusion: VCA provides a principled framework to separate true correctness from image-supported and relation-consistent reasoning, exposing hidden credit and the role of relation-specific cues in benchmark performance; null controls are insufficient to identify relation reliance, and permutation-based controls yield robust signatures of image-driven effect.

Abstract: Closed yes/no spatial benchmarks can reward a correct answer even when the image adds little support beyond no-image contexts. Under a fixed forced-choice interface, Visual Credit Audit (VCA) separates two estimands: whether the benchmark image gives the model's declared decision more support than text-only and blank controls, and whether the model responds to relation-specific visual evidence. The first audit is training- and label-free and does not require an answer flip. Applying labels yields dependence-credited correctness (D-CC); on correct items, it equals same-control gold-aligned positive gain, while prediction alignment extends the audit to errors. Across four open MLLMs and two spatial benchmarks, 12.73-26.25% of decisions are correct yet uncredited. Matched same-split image permutation reduces D-CC by 21.25-47.80 points, with every paired 95% interval above zero. Fixed-pixel relation contrasts and a 3x3 evidence-source factorial show why null controls cannot identify relation response. Among controlled correct-but-uncredited agreement decisions, response to relation reversal spans 81.57-100.00%, while 32.11% pooled change answer. Independently audited outcomes on 108 geometry-compatible edits provide a bounded natural-image correspondence check. VCA thereby decomposes benchmark success into correctness, additional image support, and relation-consistent response.

</details>


### [79] [SciFigQual-Bench: A Benchmark for Scientific Figure Quality Assessment with Full-Manuscript Context](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27084)
*Zihan Deng,Chuanzhi Xu,Huiqi Liang,Haoyang Li,Xiaozhen Zhong,Lequan Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SciFigQual-Bench: a full-text contextual benchmark for scientific images across five dimensions (clarity, layout, caption fit, context relevance, misleading risk) with 6,308 figures linked to captions and manuscript context; SFQ-Agent, a staged cross-modal evaluator, outperforms baselines using GPT-5.6-Sol, achieving low error (0.418) and high consistency (93.4%).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Address the gap that existing IQA methods on natural images do not capture scholarly figure quality aspects such as caption alignment, citation relevance, and contextual misleadingness; previous work on scholarly charts is surface-level. A contextual benchmark is needed to evaluate scientific images in real manuscript contexts.

Method: Construct SciFigQual-Bench by collecting 6,308 scientific images from top computer-science conferences (2020–2025) with gold-standard five-dimension annotations from domain experts; bind each image to its caption, citing sentence, and manuscript context. Develop SFQ-Agent, a staged cross-modal evaluation framework that aggregates modal evidence for auditable scoring. Evaluate multiple mainstream LLMs on test subset eval1200; SFQ-Agent (F3) with GPT-5.6-Sol.

Result: SFQ-Agent (F3) achieved the lowest overall average absolute error (0.418) and the highest consistency rate (93.4%), outperforming direct evaluation and Sidecar visual-language model schemes across eval1200.

Conclusion: The SciFigQual-Bench dataset and SFQ-Agent framework enable robust, auditable cross-modal evaluation of scientific figures within manuscript contexts, binding images to captions and citations, and demonstrating superior performance over baseline methods; this framework can guide future IQA research for scholarly visuals.

Abstract: Scientific images are the core elements of presenting experimental conclusions, elaborating system architecture, and supporting comparative arguments in scientific papers. However, existing image quality assessment (IQA) methods are predominantly designed for natural photographs or AI-generated content, which cannot be directly applied to scientific papers. The few existing studies on scholarly charts remain confined to visual-surface comparisons, failing to verify caption alignment, citation relevance, or visual misleadingness. To address this, we propose SciFigQual-Bench, a full-text contextual benchmark that evaluates scientific images across five dimensions (clarity, layout, caption fit, context relevance, and misleading risk). The data covers top computer-science conferences from 2020 to 2025; 6,308 images were independently scored by multiple domain experts in five dimensions and aggregated into gold-standard annotations. Unlike previous scientific figure benchmarks, our dataset binds each image to its caption, citing sentence, and manuscript context. To enable automated evaluation on this benchmark, we designed a staged cross-modal evaluation framework SFQ-Agent to achieve auditable and refined scoring through the collection and fusion of modal evidence. Multiple mainstream large models were evaluated on the test subset eval1200, and SFQ-Agent (F3) equipped with GPT-5.6-Sol achieved the lowest overall average absolute error (0.418) and the highest consistency rate (93.4%), consistently outperforming both direct evaluation and auxiliary (Sidecar) visual language model evaluation schemes.

</details>


### [80] [Step-Attention Refinement of DINOv3 Features for Efficient Anterior Eye Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27087)
*Philippe Baumstimler,Jean-Mathieu Gagnon,Sébastien Gagné,Mathieu Duchesneau,Clément Playout,Lama Séoud*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Lightweight AES segmentation model built on distilled DINOv3 ViT-Small with a step-attention refinement module, achieving strong accuracy and cross-domain robustness on clinical AES data.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Clinical anterior eye segment segmentation is hampered by heterogeneous acquisition conditions and scarce annotations; there is a need to adapt foundation-model representations efficiently to dense medical segmentation tasks.

Method: A lightweight architecture using a distilled DINOv3 ViT-Small backbone integrated with a step-attention feature refinement module that progressively refines multi-level transformer features before a convolutional decoder; evaluated with full fine-tuning against baselines on a private multi-protocol AES dataset.

Result: Achieves 85.55% mIoU under full fine-tuning, surpassing convolutional and transformer baselines including DINOv3-based methods; demonstrates strong robustness to domain shift across four unseen public AES datasets.

Conclusion: Decoder design and a progressive refinement strategy are crucial for effectively leveraging pretrained transformers in medical segmentation, establishing a robust AES segmentation baseline for clinical settings.

Abstract: Anterior eye segment (AES) segmentation is a key component of both ocular biometrics and emerging clinical image analysis applications. However, heterogeneous acquisition conditions and limited annotations in medical settings hinder the robustness and generalization of existing methods. Foundation models (FMs) such as DINOv3 offer strong transfer capabilities, but efficiently adapting their representations to dense prediction tasks remains challenging. In this study, we investigate robust AES segmentation in clinical settings, and propose a lightweight architecture built upon a distilled DINOv3 ViT-Small backbone. We introduce a step-attention feature refinement module that progressively adapts multi-level transformer representations before convolutional decoding, enabling efficient exploitation of pretrained features with few parameters. We evaluate the proposed approach on a private dataset of 333 clinically acquired AES images spanning eight ophthalmic acquisition protocols and annotated for seven anatomical classes. Compared with convolutional and transformer-based baselines, including DINOv3-based methods, our approach achieves the best overall performance, reaching 85.55\% mIoU when fully fine-tuned. It also demonstrates the strongest robustness to domain shift across four unseen public AES segmentation datasets. These results establish a strong baseline for robust AES segmentation in clinical settings and highlight the importance of decoder design for effectively adapting FMs representations to medical segmentation tasks.

</details>


### [81] [FreqForcing: Autoregressive Long Video Generation via Spectral Self-Anchoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27110)
*Jiatong Li,Leo Liang,Linghe Kong,Yulun Zhang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper analyzes autoregressive video diffusion error accumulation as low-frequency energy drift and introduces a training-free method (FreqForcing) with Spectral Self-Anchoring to stabilize long-horizon video generation, achieving significant extrapolation (24x) and competitive results against training-based methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Autoregressive diffusion models accumulate errors over long rollouts, causing color drift, stagnation, and collapse. A frequency-domain perspective motivates a lightweight, training-free approach to maintain stability without expensive retraining.

Method: Characterize error accumulation in the frequency domain; assess attention sink effects; propose FreqForcing with Spectral Self-Anchoring (SSA): anchor low-frequency attention to stabilize long horizons while preserving high-frequency dynamics via local attention. Extends Self-Forcing trained on 5s clips to ~2 minutes (24x extrapolation), using training-free SSA to reduce spectral energy drift.

Result: Empirical evidence that low-frequency energy drift correlates with quality degradation; SSA reduces, but does not fully eliminate, spectral drift; FreqForcing outperforms existing training-free methods and is competitive with training-based approaches for long-horizon video generation, including two-minute sequences.

Conclusion: Spectral Self-Anchoring provides a practical, training-free mechanism to mitigate error accumulation in autoregressive video diffusion by stabilizing low-frequency components, enabling substantial horizon extrapolation (24x). While effective, it does not completely resolve drift, indicating room for further refinement and potential hybrid approaches.

Abstract: Autoregressive video diffusion models enable real-time streaming video generation. However, errors introduced during self-rollout accumulate over long horizons, manifesting as color drift, motion stagnation, and eventual visual collapse. In this paper, we characterize this phenomenon from a frequency-domain perspective: error accumulation appears as a pronounced energy drift in the low-frequency bands. We further investigate the effectiveness of attention sink in the frequency domain, and find that it improves the video quality by alleviating the spectral energy drift to some extent, but cannot fully resolve it. Motivated by the above analysis, we propose FreqForcing, a training-free framework that addresses error accumulation in long-video generation via Spectral Self-Anchoring (SSA). The proposed SSA leverages the low-frequency components of anchor attention to maintain long-horizon visual stability, while preserving dynamic motion through the high-frequency components of local attention. Our FreqForcing extends Self-Forcing pretrained on 5s clips to two-minute generation, achieving 24x extrapolation. Extensive experiments show that FreqForcing outperforms existing training-free methods quantitatively and qualitatively while remaining competitive with representative training-based approaches.

</details>


### [82] [Veritas++: Value-aware On-Policy Distillation for Perception-Enhanced AIGI Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27113)
*Hao Tan,Jun Lan,Zichang Tan,Ajian Liu,Zijian Yu,Chuanbiao Song,Huijia Zhu,Weiqiang Wang,Jun Wan,Zhen Lei*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Veritas++ proposes perception-enhanced AIGI detection using three perception abilities; PoRL rewards-based learning; VaOPD adaptive distillation; demonstrates improved generalization and efficient capability evolution.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current MLLM-based detectors suffer from perception bottlenecks in capturing fine-grained anomalies; authenticity reasoning should be grounded in reliable perception.

Method: Three perception abilities form the foundation: fine-grained visual details, semantic anomalies, and pixel-level differences. Perception-oriented Learning (PoRL) replaces open-ended supervision with verifiable rewards. Value-aware On-Policy Distillation (VaOPD) adaptively prioritizes high-value distillation signals via a privileged self-teacher.

Result: Extensive experiments across standard, in-the-wild, and emerging benchmarks show promising generalization. Perception learning bridges the perception gap and yields gains in detection; VaOPD enables efficient capability evolution without sacrificing existing performance.

Conclusion: Veritas++ achieves strong generalization in AIGI detection; code and checkpoints are available at the provided GitHub link.

Abstract: The growing capability of image generation models has made synthetic images a routine presence in open media, making robust and generalizable AI-Generated Image (AIGI) detection increasingly essential. While multi-modal large language models (MLLMs) offer a transparent alternative to black-box binary scoring, we observe that current MLLM-based detectors still exhibit notable perception bottlenecks in capturing fine-grained anomalies. They primarily focus on how visual evidence is organized and synthesized, leaving the intrinsic perception less optimized. To mitigate this gap, we present Veritas++, a perception-enhanced reasoning framework that establishes reliable perception as the foundation of authenticity reasoning. Rather than directly optimizing the model's explanatory ability, we ground AIGI detection on three basic perception abilities, i.e., capturing fine-grained visual details, semantic anomalies and pixel-level differences. Building on this insight, we introduce Perception-oriented Learning (PoRL), which replaces open-ended description supervision with verifiable rewards to explicitly strengthen these capacities. To further integrate enhanced perception with reasoning, we introduce Value-aware On-Policy Distillation (VaOPD), an adaptive distillation mechanism that prioritizes high-value distillation signals over uniform supervision, internalizing perception-aware reasoning through a privileged self-teacher. Extensive experiments across standard, in-the-wild and emerging benchmarks demonstrate that Veritas++ achieves promising generalization. The perception learning effectively bridges the perception gap and yields seamless gains on detection, while VaOPD further enables efficient capability evolvement without sacrificing existing performance. Code and checkpoints are available at https://github.com/EricTan7/VeritasPP.

</details>


### [83] [Towards Grounded GI Endoscopy VQA via Multi-Task Learning on Small VLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27122)
*Itbaan Safwan,Ramail Khan,Muhammad Annas Shaikh,Muhammad Atif Tahir*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A simple multi-task fine-tuning method improves grounding alignment and VQA accuracy for GI endoscopy VLMs by reusing expert polyp masks and Grad-CAM-based weak supervision to create auxiliary grounding/description tasks, applied with LoRA fine-tuning on Kvasir-VQA-x1.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Bridge the gap between high answer accuracy and interpretable visual evidence in clinical VQA, enabling models to align answers with corresponding image regions without extensive extra annotations.

Method: Fine-tune three small vision-language backbones using low-rank adaptation under two recipes (VQA-only and multi-task). Use expert polyp masks directly as ground-truth for grounding; for categories lacking masks, leverage Grad-CAM localization from a GI-domain classifier to provide weak supervision. Train on Kvasir-VQA-x1 with the auxiliary tasks derived from an existing VQA dataset, comparing performance and implicit answer–region alignment.

Result: Multi-task fine-tuning yields consistent accuracy gains over the VQA-only baseline on both in-distribution and out-of-distribution data, and improves implicit alignment between answer tokens and relevant image regions.

Conclusion: Auxiliary grounding and description tasks derived from existing annotations and weak Grad-CAM signals can enhance both performance and interpretability of GI endoscopy VLMs with modest annotation effort, suggesting practical path toward clinically accountable VQA systems.

Abstract: Gastrointestinal (GI) endoscopic image analysis has shifted from single-label classification toward visual question answering (VQA), where a model must answer free-form clinical questions about an image. While recent vision-language models (VLMs) achieve promising answer accuracy on this task, clinical adoption also requires the model's internal representations to reflect the visual evidence behind its answers. We propose a simple multi-task fine-tuning recipe that constructs auxiliary grounding and description tasks from an existing VQA dataset with minimal additional annotation: expert-annotated polyp masks are reused directly, while a GI-domain pretrained classifier with Grad-CAM localization provides weak supervision for finding categories that lack ground-truth masks. Three small VLM backbones are fine-tuned with low-rank adaptation under matched VQA-only and multi-task recipes on Kvasir-VQA-x1, and we show consistent accuracy gains together with improved implicit alignment between answer tokens and the relevant image region, evaluated on both in-distribution and out-of-distribution data.

</details>


### [84] [SeasonStereo: Robust Dense Stereo Matching for Multi-Date Satellite Imagery via Generative AI](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27139)
*Álvaro Díaz-Laureano,Roger Marí,Elías Masquil,Pablo Arias,Gabriele Facciolo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SeasonStereo uses synthetic diachronic image pairs and foundation-model priors to train robust disparity estimation for satellite imagery, enabling LiDAR-level 3D reconstruction without real multi-date labels.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Diachronic satellite imagery suffers from appearance changes due to seasons and lighting, making dense stereo matching difficult and costly because aligned multi-date data and ground-truth geometry are hard to obtain at scale.

Method: Train disparity networks on synthetic image pairs with controlled seasonal variation, and incorporate zero-shot geometric priors from foundation models to guide geometry without requiring real multi-date training data or LiDAR labels.

Result: Achieves LiDAR-supervised-level accuracy while producing sharper geometric details, without relying on aligned real multi-date training data or LiDAR-derived labels, enabling scalable 3D reconstruction from heterogeneous satellite images.

Conclusion: SeasonStereo provides a practical, low-supervision pathway for large-scale 3D reconstruction from diachronic satellite imagery by combining synthetic seasonal variation training with foundation-model priors to match state-of-the-art geometry.

Abstract: Accurate 3D reconstruction from satellite imagery typically relies on near-simultaneous stereo pairs, limiting its applicability to diachronic settings where multi-date images exhibit varying seasonal and illumination conditions. Training dense stereo matching models robust to appearance changes is a long-standing challenge, as aligned multi-date imagery and ground-truth geometry are costly to obtain at scale. We propose SeasonStereo, a scalable framework that addresses disparity estimation from diachronic satellite images by training on synthetic image pairs with controlled seasonal appearance variation, while leveraging zero-shot geometric priors from foundation models. SeasonStereo matches the accuracy of state-of-the-art LiDAR-supervised models, while producing sharper geometric details without requiring aligned real multi-date training products or LiDAR-derived labels. As a result, SeasonStereo offers a practical path toward large-scale 3D reconstruction from heterogeneous satellite images with reduced supervision cost.

</details>


### [85] [Explainable and Resource-Efficient Spatial Reasoning in Multimodal LLMs for Decision-Critical Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27145)
*Piyush Jain,Kousik Dasgupta,Rajarshi Roy,Subarna Tripathi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ByDeWay-V2 adds explicit spatial predicates derived from pairwise object relations and depth cues to MLLM prompts, enabling auditable, training-free spatial reasoning and improving performance on VSR/BLINK with modest resources.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To address opacity and object hallucination in MLLMs' spatial judgments by moving beyond coarse depth layering to explicit, human-readable spatial relations.

Method: Use an open-vocabulary detector (YOLO-World-L) to compute pairwise geometric relations between detected objects, inject structured spatial predicates into the MLLM prompt along with depth cues, without any training; evaluate on VSR and BLINK across multiple MLLMs; perform POPE for grounding; run within a 40-token CPU budget.

Result: On BLINK spatial subset, 46% relative F1 improvement over LDP for Qwen2.5-VL; on VSR, recovers BLIP-Base's spatial reasoning from near-random to F1 0.53; hardware-light configuration supports real-time use.

Conclusion: Explicit spatial predicates bridge 3D depth and 2D semantics, enabling auditable, training-free spatial reasoning in MLLMs, with strong benchmark gains and practical resource efficiency.

Abstract: As Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) are increasingly deployed in decision-critical pipelines such as robotics, embodied AI, and safety monitoring, the opacity of their spatial judgments limits operator trust and auditability. MLLMs demonstrate strong reasoning but often struggle with fine-grained spatial understanding and object hallucination. Prior work, ByDeWay, introduced Layered-Depth-Based Prompting (LDP), a training-free framework that mitigates hallucinations by structuring prompts using monocular depth estimation. However, coarse depth layering falls short in resolving object-to-object spatial relationships within the same geometric plane, such as projective ("left of", "above") and topological ("inside", "touching") relations. We propose ByDeWay-V2, which integrates explicit spatial relational context alongside depth cues, expressed as human-readable predicates that serve as auditable evidence for downstream decision support. Using an open-vocabulary object detector (YOLO-World-L), our framework computes pairwise geometric relations between detected objects and injects them as structured spatial predicates into the MLLM prompt, bridging 3D scene depth and 2D spatial semantics without any training. We evaluate ByDeWay-V2 on the Visual Spatial Reasoning (VSR) and BLINK benchmarks across multiple MLLMs, with hallucination grounding assessed via POPE. On the BLINK spatial subset, ByDeWay-V2 achieves a 46 percent relative F1 improvement over LDP for Qwen2.5-VL, and recovers BLIP-Base's spatial reasoning on VSR from near-random performance to a competitive F1 of 0.53. Our lightest configuration operates under a strict 40-token context budget on CPU, showing the framework's suitability for resource-constrained, real-time decision-support settings.

</details>


### [86] [Anatomy Contextualized Adaption of CT Foundation Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.27154)
*Roshan Kenia,Stephanie L McNamara,William Lotter*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A lightweight framework (ACA) that adapts frozen CT vision-language foundations to anatomy-level alignment while preserving global context, achieving strong zero-shot performance with minimal training.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Fine-grained vision-language pre-training aligns anatomy-level visual features with anatomy-specific text but often discards global context from whole-volume models. Training fine-grained approaches from scratch is computationally expensive.

Method: Anatomy Contextualized Adaptation (ACA) decomposes CT volumes into anatomy-level embeddings using TotalSegmentator, refines them with a cross-anatomy transformer to capture inter-anatomy relationships, and aligns them to per-anatomy and scan-level text from radiology reports. Embeddings are cached; the model is trained for under an hour.

Result: ACA outperforms both frozen foundation-model baselines and existing fine-grained methods in zero-shot finding classification on Merlin and CT-RATE. Training time is under one hour after embeddings are cached; attention weights indicate plausible cross-anatomy context routing.

Conclusion: ACA provides a lightweight, context-preserving approach for adapting CT foundation models to anatomically grounded vision-language alignment, yielding strong zero-shot performance with minimal training and clear cross-anatomy interpretability through attention.

Abstract: CT vision-language foundation models have demonstrated promising performance across downstream tasks, but are typically trained with whole-volume representations that dilute fine-grained anatomical signals. Fine-grained vision-language pre-training addresses this by aligning anatomy-level visual features with anatomy-specific text, but in doing so discards the global context that whole-volume models provide. Furthermore, existing fine-grained approaches train from scratch, making them computationally expensive. We introduce Anatomy Contextualized Adaptation (ACA), a lightweight framework that adapts frozen CT foundation model representations for anatomy-level vision-language alignment while enhancing global contextualization. ACA uses TotalSegmentator to decompose CT volumes into anatomy-level embeddings, which are refined via a transformer that captures cross-anatomy relationships, and aligned to both per-anatomy and scan-level text extracted from radiology reports. Evaluated on Merlin and CT-RATE, ACA consistently outperforms both the frozen foundation model baselines and existing fine-grained methods in zero-shot finding classification, while requiring less than one hour of training once embeddings are cached. The attention weights learned by ACA's inter-anatomy transformer additionally indicate plausible cross-anatomy context routing. Altogether, these results support ACA as a lightweight approach for adapting CT foundation models to anatomically grounded vision-language alignment while preserving and enhancing global anatomical context.

</details>


<div id='cs.RO'></div>

# cs.RO [[Back]](#toc)

### [87] [Towards Trustworthy Embodied Intelligence: A Systems Framework and Graded Trustworthiness Levels](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26121)
*Xinyu Yang,Tianxing Chen,Honghao Su,Minxuan Wang,Chenze Yu,Zhangzheng Tu,Yue Chen,Yuxiao Huo,Lingfeng Zhang,Yan Huang,Yan Qin,Shaolong Zhu,Qiwei Liang,Hekun Tian,Shujia Liu,Guangyu Chen,Junhao Gong,Zixuan Li,Wenwei Lin,Zijian Lin,Wenxuan Zhu,Eric J Chen,Yue Yuan,Qize Yu,Jiaqi Liang,Haowen Yan,Hengfei Zhao,Weijie Wan,Zikun Xiao,Junyuan Tang,Baijun Chen,Kai-Chong Lei,Kaixuan Wang,Kailun Su,Zanxin Chen,Yao Mu,Renjing Xu,Chuqiao Lyu,Qi Xiong,Ping Luo,Wenbo Ding*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Defines trustworthy embodied intelligence as the sustained capacity to reliably complete tasks under environmental and system variation while maintaining risk within bounds, organized into four interdependent layers (model, system, evidence, deployment) and a non-normative hierarchy of trustworthiness levels for bounded deployment and evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To ensure safety and reliability of embodied AI systems where failures can cause physical or operational harm, task success alone is insufficient for trust. The paper argues for end-to-end trustworthiness across architecture, assurance, and governance layers.

Method: Proposes a conceptual framework with four interdependent layers: model (uncertainty calibration and safety preferences), system (authorized actions via sensing, computation, control, safeguards, fault containment, fallback), evidence (evaluation, verification, validation, traceability, assurance arguments), deployment (runtime monitoring, authority management, intervention, incident response, controlled updates). It also introduces a non-normative hierarchy of trustworthiness levels spanning task capability, safety, system assurance, operational governance, and supporting evidence.

Result: A structured architecture for trustworthiness in embodied AI, including explicit layers and components, plus a graded trustworthiness hierarchy to guide bounded deployment, comparative evaluation, and standardization.

Conclusion: End-to-end trustworthiness cannot be achieved by improving a single component; a holistic, cross-layer approach with bounded deployment claims and a hierarchy of trustworthiness levels is needed, enabling evaluation, prioritization, and future standardization.

Abstract: Embodied intelligence integrates learned perception and decision making with real-time computation, control, and physical interaction. Because failures can cause immediate physical or operational harm, task completion alone does not establish trustworthiness. We define trustworthy embodied intelligence as the sustained capacity to execute specified tasks reliably under environmental and system variation while maintaining risk within acceptable bounds. We term this objective sustained safe success. Its supporting mechanisms are organized into four interdependent layers. The model layer generates task-competent action proposals with calibrated uncertainty and explicit safety preferences. The system layer realizes authorized actions dependably through integrated sensing, computation, control, hardware safeguards, fault containment, and fallback. The evidence layer substantiates bounded claims through evaluation, verification, validation, traceability, and structured assurance arguments. The deployment layer maintains claim validity through runtime monitoring, authority management, intervention, incident response, and controlled updates. Because assumptions and failures propagate across these layers, neither model capability, isolated safeguards, nor benchmark performance alone can establish end-to-end trustworthiness. Drawing on embodied AI, robotics, control, dependable computing, distributed systems, and autonomous driving, we further propose a non-normative hierarchy of trustworthiness levels. This hierarchy grades the strength of bounded deployment claims across task capability, safety, system assurance, operational governance, and supporting evidence, providing a basis for bounded deployment, comparative evaluation, research prioritization, and future standardization.

</details>


### [88] [Embodied Agents Take Control: Minimal-Interface Zero-Shot Agents Rival Industrial-Scale Policies in Vision-and-Language Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26148)
*Jian Zhou,Xunyi Zhao,Gengze Zhou,Zerui Li,Sihao Lin,Jiajun Liu,Qi Wu*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Agentic embodied control uses a general-purpose agent to manage its perception-action loop, evaluated on zero-shot navigation with monocular RGB input. Three harnesses are tested; adding a waypoint tool improves performance for weaker models. Results show competitive success for short horizons, but performance degrades on longer horizons due to latency and context growth. Models, harnesses, and interfaces contribute complementary advantages.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To explore whether a general-purpose agent that self-manages its decision loop can achieve autonomous embodied navigation without task-specific pipelines, and to compare different software-engineering harnesses and interfaces.

Method: Zero-shot navigation with a monocular RGB camera and discrete actions; evaluate three agent-harness families; measure success across default vs maximum effort; introduce an optional trained waypoint tool; perform controlled interventions to assess model-centered effects; analyze success rates, steps, wall time, latency, and context growth.

Result: Default-effort configurations reach roughly 70.7% (opus-5) and 78% (fable-5) at maximum effort; with the waypoint tool present, fable-5 reaches 76.7% at default effort, using about half the environment steps and less than a quarter of wall time of maximum-effort primitive runs. Controlled interventions show model choice dominates outcomes; harness effects are mainly descriptive; forced waypoint interfaces aid weaker models but can hinder stronger ones.

Conclusion: Agentic control is already competitive for zero-shot navigation; models, harnesses, and interfaces offer complementary paths to autonomous embodied agents. Performance declines on longer horizons, and latency/context growth limit sustained operation, indicating areas for improvement.

Abstract: Autonomous embodied agents must sustain a long decision-making loop that involves perceiving, acting, verifying, and self-correcting over many steps. Current systems sustain this loop through task-specific workflows or embodied policies. We study a third form, agentic embodied control, in which a general-purpose agent holds the loop itself. Using zero-shot navigation as a controlled testbed, we evaluate three software-engineering agent harnesses given only a monocular RGB camera and discrete actions. Under this strictly minimal condition, replicated default-effort configurations reach 70.7$\pm$3.5% success (opus-5, mean over three runs), and fable-5 reaches 78% at maximum effort. When a trained waypoint tool is exposed alongside primitives as an optional capability, the hybrid fable-5 agent reaches 76.7$\pm$0.6% at default effort, using half the environment steps and less than one quarter of the wall time of the maximum-effort primitive run. Controlled interventions show that capability is primarily model-centered: model choice strongly changes success, harness effects are descriptive, and a forced waypoint interface helps weaker models but can hinder stronger ones. Performance nevertheless falls sharply on longer-horizon tasks, while latency and context growth limit sustained operation. These results show that agentic control is already competitive in zero-shot navigation and that models, harnesses, and interfaces offer complementary paths toward autonomous embodied agents.

</details>


### [89] [Multi-Objective Compliance-Integrated Coevolution For Simulated And Real-World Deployment Of Multi-Robot Marine Autonomy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26279)
*Everardo Gonzalez,Tyler M. Paine,Manuel Agraz Vallejo,Gaurav Dixit,Michael R. Benjamin,Kagan Tumer*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A multi-objective coevolution framework blends coevolved behaviors with compliance to norms for marine multi-robot coordination; decouples learning from compliance; demonstrated on marine rescue with up to 8 real vehicles and 12 in simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Need to balance maximizing team progress with adherence to predefined operational norms and safety constraints in sparse-feedback, multi-robot maritime missions.

Method: Marine Multi-Objective Compliance-Integrated Coevolution (MMOCIC): decouples learning from compliance; integrates team-wide optimization with established norms; combines coevolutionary learning for coordination with a normative compliance module; validated in hardware (up to 8 vehicles) and simulation (12 vehicles).

Result: Achieves high team performance while minimizing norm violations; avoids collisions in collaborative swimmer rescue missions; validated on eight-vehicle hardware deployment and twelve-vehicle simulation.

Conclusion: MMOCIC offers a practical framework to embed normative compliance within learning-based coordination for real-world maritime multi-robot operations; demonstrates that decoupling learning from compliance can preserve performance while enhancing safety.

Abstract: Collaborative robots are well-suited to maritime missions that benefit from coordination, such as the exploration of unknown reef structures, inspection of subsea infrastructure, or search-and-rescue operations. These missions typically provide sparse feedback signals for measuring progress and require adherence to safety and regulatory norms, turning a mission into a multi-objective optimization problem. Coevolutionary algorithms can process these sparse feedback signals to generate coordinated behaviors, and in some cases extend behaviors to multiple objectives. However, incorporating high-level team objectives with low-level compliance considerations on the fly to balance norm adherence with team performance remains elusive. This paper introduces a multi-objective framework that blends coevolved behaviors with compliance behaviors to achieve a balance between maximizing team progress and minimizing norm violations. The key insight is to decouple learning from compliance since operational norms are prescribed rather than discovered. We demonstrate that our framework achieves high team performance while avoiding collisions on a collaborative swimmer rescue mission with up to 8 vehicles in a hardware deployment, and 12 vehicles in simulation. The key contribution of this paper is Marine Multi-Objective Compliance-Integrated Coevolution (MMOCIC), a framework that blends team-wide optimization with established norms for real-world deployments of learning-based coordination.

</details>


### [90] [MoMo: Dial Motion Mode in Robot Manipulation with Spatiotemporal Action Tokenization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26315)
*Yuhan Hu,Hugues Thomas,Peide Huang,Mouli Sivapurapu,Benoit Landry,Arto Kivila*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: MoMo is a two-stage imitation-learning framework that learns a reusable motion-mode (execution style) factor for manipulation tasks via a spatiotemporal action tokenizer and a behavior-cloning transformer conditioned on task and motion mode, enabling compositional generalization across tasks and unseen modes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robots must adapt how actions unfold across contexts (task, object, interaction setting). The study investigates whether execution-level variation can be learned as a reusable behavioral factor shared across tasks to improve generalization and adaptation.

Method: MoMo comprises two stages: (1) a spatiotemporal action tokenizer that encodes actions over space and time, and (2) a behavior-cloning transformer that generates motion conditioned on the task and a continuous motion-mode input. Trained on six real-robot manipulation tasks with varying motion modes, and evaluated for qualitative and quantitative differences in execution (joint speed, acceleration, end-effector pitch).

Result: Varying the motion-mode condition yields steady, dynamic, and intermediate behaviors that human raters can distinguish; these modes affect kinematics (speed, acceleration, pitch). On tasks demonstrated in only one mode, MoMo can transfer an unseen requested mode while largely preserving task success. This demonstrates compositional generalization to unseen task–mode combinations and reuse of motion mode across tasks to control how skills are performed.

Conclusion: Motion mode operates as a reusable, composable execution-factor that can be conditioned into a manipulation policy to customize how skills are performed. MoMo provides evidence of cross-task generalization and execution-level adaptability, enabling flexible, task-agnostic control of manipulation styles.

Abstract: To operate effectively across diverse contexts, robots must not only perform manipulation tasks accurately but also adapt how their actions unfold to the task, object, and interaction setting. We ask whether this execution-level variation can be learned as a reusable behavioral factor shared across tasks. We present \textbf{MoMo}, a two-stage imitation-learning framework consisting of a spatiotemporal action tokenizer and a behavior-cloning transformer that takes task and a continuous motion-mode condition as inputs. Across six real-robot manipulation tasks, varying this condition produces steady, dynamic, and intermediate behaviors that human raters can distinguish and that differ in joint speed, acceleration, and end-effector approach pitch. On tasks demonstrated in only one mode, MoMo transfers the unseen requested mode while largely preserving task success. Together, these results provide evidence of compositional generalization to unseen task--mode combinations and show that motion mode can be reused across tasks to control how a manipulation skill is performed.

</details>


### [91] [Reeling It In: Flexible Needle Pick Up via Thread Manipulation for Autonomous Suturing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26337)
*Emma Huang,Zih-Yun Chiu,Neelay Joglekar,Shanglei Liu,Michael C. Yip*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Autonomous suture-needle pickup is achieved using a suture thread as an assistive tool to handle occluded/inaccessible needles, with a full pipeline and robustness to visual uncertainty, demonstrated on a dVRK.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current autonomous needle pickup relies on direct straight-line grasping, risking tissue contact and failing when the needle is occluded. A thread-assisted approach aims to improve safety and robustness in unstructured suturing.

Method: Develops a complete framework: thread and tissue reconstruction, safe grasp-point selection, stable thread lifting, and bimanual thread-following to achieve needle grasping without direct contact; policies robust to visual uncertainty; evaluated on dVRK.

Result: Robust performance under challenging thread configurations or non-approachable needles, closing the gap for autonomous suturing in unstructured environments.

Conclusion: Thread-assisted autonomous pickup enables safer, more robust needle grasping in challenging scenarios and broadens applicability of autonomous suturing policies.

Abstract: Suture-needle pickup is necessary for autonomous suturing, as a needle can be unexpectedly dropped or strategically released to adjust the grasping configuration. Current methods for autonomous needle pickup typically guide a robot to move straight toward the needle and grasp it, limited to conditions where the needle is observable and directly approachable. In addition, grasping the needle lying on tissue can lead to the robot pinching nearby tissue or the needle jumping around due to its slippery surface, posing potential safety issues. This work proposes an autonomous framework that uses a suture thread as an assistive tool for indirect needle pickup, avoiding unnecessary tool-tissue contact and enabling pickup even when the needle is occluded or inaccessible. The framework spans the entire workflow, including thread and tissue reconstruction, safe grasp-point selection, stable thread lifting, and bimanual thread-following until securing needle grasping. The robot policies account for visual uncertainty to maximize robustness in real-world environments. We evaluate the proposed framework on a da Vinci Research Kit under various real-world conditions. The results demonstrate robust performance even with a challenging thread configuration or a non-approachable needle, closing the gap in applying autonomous robot policies to unstructured suturing environments.

</details>


### [92] [Self-Adaptive Learning and Model Predictive Control for Tracking Unknown Dynamics with No Regret](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26370)
*Atharva Navsalkar,Hongyu Zhou,Vasileios Tzoumas*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Online self-adaptive predictor-selection for tracking unknown switching target dynamics with finite-time regret guarantees; self-supervised, one-shot learning; validated on Crazyflie; outperforms baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Handle tracking of targets with unknown, switching dynamics (structured, random, adversarial) in robotics; provide robustness, efficiency, and guarantees beyond RFF-based online learning.

Method: Maintain multiple predictors trained from scratch via self-supervised one-shot learning; online select the best predictor to match observed behavior; derive finite-time near-optimality in expectation as a function of target-learning error and switch frequency; no-regret in the absence of error and switching; graceful degradation under learning errors and switching; novel technical approach relative to RFF-based methods.

Result: Finite-time near-optimality in expectation; asymptotic no-regret with perfect knowledge; graceful degradation when errors or switching occur; empirical validation on Crazyflie simulations and hardware with diverse target dynamics; outperforms non-stochastic, kernel-based, and neural-network-based online learning methods.

Conclusion: The proposed framework delivers robust online control performance under switching target dynamics with rigorous guarantees and practical validation, offering a competitive alternative to existing RFF-based, kernel-based, or neural-network-based online learning approaches; suitable for dynamic mapping, traffic control, and pursuit-evasion tasks.

Abstract: We propose a self-adaptive online learning for control method for tracking unknown target dynamics. The target dynamics can exhibit switching behavior, particularly, a mixture of structured, random, and/or adversarial motion. Such challenging target tracking scenarios arise in applications of dynamic mapping, traffic control, and pursuit evasion, where robots need to track, pursue, or avoid collision with moving landmarks, objects, humans, etc., whose dynamics are unknown. Our method simultaneously learns multiple predictors from scratch, via self-supervised, one-shot, and computationally efficient learning, and adaptively selects the best one to match the observed target behavior. The method enjoys finite-time near-optimality guarantees in expectation, characterized as a function of the learning error of the target dynamics and the frequency that the target dynamics switch. In the absence of both error and switching, the method asymptotically matches the optimal non-causal control policy that knows a priori the target dynamics, i.e., the method enjoys no regret in expectation. In the presence of learning errors and switching, the method degrades gracefully, \eg when there are errors and no switching, the average regret is proportional to the average learning error and switching times. To prove these guarantees, a novel technical approach is required compared to the existing works that employ RFF-based online learning. We validate our method in Crazyflie simulations and hardware experiments, across target trajectories that vary from structured to random to adversarial, in comparison to non-stochastic, kernel-based, and neural-network-based methods for online learning.

</details>


### [93] [Reinforcement Learning on Cost-Constrained Quadrupedal Hardware](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26434)
*Javier C. Weddington,Bence P. Ölveczky,Stephen A. Baccus*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A transport-delay-aware, time-aware policy with a learned CPG boosts robustness of locomotion on a low-cost quadruped by mitigating sim-to-real gaps and tolerating large latency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transport delays and noisy motor feedback on inexpensive hardware turn the planning/control problem into partially observable, widening the sim-to-real gap for learned policies.

Method: 1) Model average actuator delay to form a forward model; 2) a time-aware neural network that conditions on time delay; 3) learn a central pattern generator (CPG) for rhythmic gait; 4) validate on Mini Pupper 2 hardware with latency perturbations up to ~320 ms.

Result: The approach yields robust locomotion despite delay, with a CPG-based policy maintaining stable gaits under substantial latency; demonstrates improved sim-to-real transfer on cost-constrained hardware.

Conclusion: Temporal self-organization via delay-aware modeling and CPG-like rhythms is a promising, general strategy to close the sim-to-real gap for low-cost locomotion systems.

Abstract: Deploying learned control policies on low-cost robotic platforms introduces transport latencies and noisy motor feedback that systematically widens the sim-to-real gap. The chasm of simulation to deployment in hardware lies in the delay of the actuator reaching the commanded position. On platforms such as the Mini Pupper 2, a measured > $50 ms transport delay transforms the locomotion task from a standard Markov decision process into a partially observable one. In this paper, we take a biologically inspired approach of handling noisy and delayed feedback to close the sim-to-real gap, thereby expanding the capability of reinforcement learning on cost-constrained hardware. Using a low-cost quadrupedal hardware platform, we find that using a forward model of the average actuator delay, paired with a time-aware neural network results in robust locomotion. Additionally, our time-aware neural network learned a central pattern generator (CPG): a self-sustaining rhythmic gait that is robust to +320 ms latency perturbations, mirroring the CPGs found in the spinal cords of vertebrates. We posit that temporal self-organization may be a general strategy for cost-constrained locomotion.

</details>


### [94] [RLMM-Flow: A Flow-based Mobile Manipulation Framework with Latent-Space Reinforcement Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26460)
*Shuhang Wang,Ziming Li,Hui Cheng*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Proposes RLMM-Flow: a flow-based mobile manipulation policy trained via expert demonstrations with latent RL post-training to improve performance while maintaining fast inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Imitation-only training limits policy quality and multimodal, temporally consistent motion priors are needed for robust mobile manipulation under base kinematic constraints and joint limits.

Method: 1) Learn a multimodal whole-body motion prior via flow-based policy from expert demonstrations. 2) Freeze the pretrained flow policy. 3) Use a latent steering network to steer the initial noise toward higher-value action chunks. 4) Stabilize high-dimensional latent optimization with an action-space critic warm-up. 5) Jointly train latent critic and latent actor. 6) Employ coarse-to-fine latent steering to expand control from a horizon-shared latent representation to a full-dimensional residual representation.

Result: Empirical results on mobile manipulation motion-planning benchmarks show RLMM-Flow substantially improves task success, collision avoidance, and trajectory quality over imitation-only flow policies and existing RL post-training baselines, while preserving fast flow-based inference.

Conclusion: RLMM-Flow effectively integrates expert flow-policy pretraining with latent-space reinforcement learning to enhance performance while maintaining efficient inference.

Abstract: Mobile manipulation requires generating whole-body action chunks that jointly satisfy goal reaching, collision avoidance, base kinematic constraints, manipulator joint limits, and trajectory smoothness. Flow-based generative policies provide an efficient paradigm for learning multimodal and temporally consistent motion priors from expert demonstrations, but imitation-only training cannot improve policy quality beyond the demonstration distribution. We propose RLMM-Flow, a flow-based mobile manipulation framework that combines expert flow-policy pretraining with latent-space reinforcement learning post-training. The framework first learns a flow policy that captures a multimodal whole-body motion prior from expert demonstrations. The pretrained flow policy is then frozen, while a latent steering network steers its initial noise toward higher-value action chunks. To stabilize high-dimensional latent optimization, we warm up an action-space critic before jointly training the latent critic and latent actor, and introduce coarse-to-fine latent steering that progressively expands control from a horizon-shared latent representation to a full-dimensional residual representation. Experiments on mobile manipulation motion-planning benchmarks show that RLMM-Flow substantially improves task success, collision avoidance, and trajectory quality over imitation-only flow policies and existing reinforcement learning post-training baselines, while preserving fast flow-based inference.

</details>


### [95] [Explicit Kinematic Guidance from Analytic Concepts for Vision-Language-Action Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26513)
*Mingyang Sun,Jiude Wei,Xiujian Liang,Qichen He,Donglin Wang,Cewu Lu,Jianhua Sun*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: Two-phase Concept Expert for Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models uses 3D information and Analytic Concepts to guide manipulation; improves success and learning efficiency.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current VLA models rely on 2D inputs, missing 3D structural knowledge and commonsense physics, limiting spatial awareness and high-precision manipulation.

Method: Phase 1 (pre-inference): leverage Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) to estimate initial kinematic/structural parameters. Phase 2: during manipulation, dynamically track concept parameters and align with observations; use Analytic Concepts to provide dense programmatic rewards and precise spatial guidance for fine-tuning.

Result: Consistent improvements in success rate and learning efficiency across supervised and reinforcement learning settings, demonstrating effective concept-based guidance for post-training VLA.

Conclusion: Integrating Analytic Concepts via a Concept Expert yields physically grounded VLA behavior while preserving end-to-end learning flexibility.

Abstract: Current Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models rely mainly on 2D inputs, neglecting the rich object structural information and commonsense knowledge inherent in the 3D physical world. This deficiency restricts their spatial awareness and adaptability for complex, high-precision manipulation. To bridge this crucial gap, we construct a Concept Expert module for VLA to build executable Analytic Concepts that represent objects as explicit, programmatic blueprints. Our mechanism operates in two synergistic phases: First, prior to VLA inference, the Concept Expert leverages 3D information from Vision Foundation Models (VFMs) to estimate the initial kinematic and structural parameters. Second, throughout the manipulation process, the VLA model utilizes its inherent capability to dynamically track the dynamic concept parameters, continuously aligning them with observational changes to ensure persistent accuracy. Once established, the Analytic Concepts provide explicit, high-quality guidance for VLA fine-tuning through (1) dense, programmatic manipulation rewards and (2) precise spatial guidance.
  This formulation allows VLA models to learn physically grounded interaction behaviors while maintaining end-to-end learning flexibility.
  Our experimental results show consistent improvements in success rate and learning efficiency across supervised and reinforcement learning settings, demonstrating the effectiveness of structured, concept-based guidance for VLA post-training.

</details>


### [96] [Speech2Grasp: Data-Efficient Transfer of Text-Conditioned Grasp Detection to Speech in Humanoid Robots](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26567)
*Hung Nguyen,Kim Nhat Minh Nguyen,Van Duc Vu,Van-Danh Le,Hoang Huy Le,Dinh Tuan Nguyen,Pham Tuyen Le,Van-Truong Nguyen,Quan Nguyen*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A lightweight MLP projector adapts a text-conditioned vision-language model (ALBEF) to speech, enabling data-efficient transfer for speech-based grasp detection (Speech2Grasp) that outperforms ASR pipelines and reduces latency in real-world humanoid robots.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: To enable natural, speech-based interaction for robots by leveraging established text-conditioned models, reducing data requirements and latency compared to traditional ASR+vision pipelines.

Method: Diagnostic analyses of ALBEF with a lightweight MLP-based projector to map speech representations into the model’s input space; development of Speech2Grasp, a data-efficient transfer framework for speech-conditioned grasp detection; evaluation on real-world humanoid robot experiments comparing against cascaded ASR-based pipelines.

Result: The MLP-based projector preserves semantic discrimination and robustness when adapting to speech; Speech2Grasp achieves better performance than a cascaded ASR pipeline and reduces inference latency in real-world tests.

Conclusion: A practical paradigm for extending established text-conditioned systems to speech, enabling data-efficient transfer for speech-conditioned perception tasks in robotics.

Abstract: Humanoid robots increasingly require multi-modal understanding for natural interaction with humans. Despite the prominence of vision-language models, they generally assume textual rather than the more natural speech inputs. In this paper, we investigate whether a well-established text-conditioned model can be transferred to speech in a data-efficient manner. Using ALBEF as a case study, we conduct diagnostic analyses showing that a lightweight MLP-based projector effectively adapts it to speech, while preserving semantic discrimination and robustness. Motivated by these findings, we introduce Speech2Grasp, a framework for data-efficient transfer of text-conditioned grasp detection to speech. Real-world humanoid robot experiments show that Speech2Grasp outperforms cascaded ASR-based pipeline, while reducing inference latency. Our findings suggest a practical paradigm for extending established text-conditioned systems to speech.

</details>


### [97] [Semi-Decentralized Multi-Spacecraft Collision Avoidance under Communication Constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.26570)
*Grace Ra Kim,Mahdi Al-Husseini,Duncan Eddy,Mykel J. Kochenderfer*

Main category: cs.RO

TL;DR: A semi-decentralized POMDP framework with ground-station–driven information flow for spacecraft collision avoidance; uses RS-SDA* to compute joint policies; achieves near-centralized performance with 28.5% fewer synchronization events under intermittent communication, outperforming rule-based heuristics; bridges centralized and decentralized planning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Operational collision avoidance faces intermittent ground-station communication, causing delays and asynchronous information that degrade centralized performance. The work aims to quantify and optimize coordination under realistic communication constraints.

Method: Model collision avoidance as a semi-decentralized POMDP (SDec-POMDP) with information propagation constrained by ground-station visibility windows. Compute joint maneuvers using approximate Recursive Small-Step Semi-Decentralized A* (RS-SDA*), an A*-based planning lineage for decentralized multiagent systems.

Result: Semi-decentralized planning achieves near-centralized maneuver quality while reducing synchronization events by 28.5% relative to continuous coordination. Compared to rule-based heuristics, the approach more reliably maintains the desired miss-distance band and minimizes trajectory deviation.

Conclusion: The framework provides a practical, communication-aware planning approach for autonomous collision avoidance under intermittent connectivity, bridging centralized coordination and fully decentralized execution.

Abstract: Current spacecraft collision-avoidance operations rely on intermittent ground-station contacts, requiring operators to plan with delayed and asynchronously updated information. Consequently, maneuvers must be planned with only intermittent information sharing between operators, raising the question of how much coordination is needed to achieve collision-avoidance performance comparable to centralized planning. Although decision-theoretic approaches such as partially observable Markov decision processes (POMDPs) capture the sequential and uncertain nature of collision avoidance, existing multiagent extensions typically assume either continuous information sharing or communication models that do not reflect operational ground-station constraints. To explicitly model this intermittent information availability, we formulate the spacecraft-to-spacecraft collision avoidance problem as a semi-decentralized POMDP (SDec-POMDP), where we govern information propagation directly by realistic ground-station visibility windows. Joint maneuver policies are computed using approximate Recursive Small-Step Semi-Decentralized A* (RS-SDA*), following the state-of-the-art A*-based lineage for decentralized multiagent planning. Across a representative suite of conjunction scenarios, semi-decentralized planning recovers near-centralized maneuver quality while requiring 28.5% fewer synchronization events than continuous coordination. Comparisons with representative rule-based operator heuristics further show that communication-aware planning more consistently achieves the desired operational miss-distance band while minimizing unnecessary trajectory deviation. Together, these results establish a practical planning framework for autonomous collision avoidance under realistic intermittent communication, bridging the gap between idealized centralized coordination and fully decentralized planning execution.

</details>
